[AMENDED 2026-11-03, Story 33.1, orchestrator unattended ruling, veto U-246]
  - **The agent heartbeat** [AMENDED 2026-10-31, owner decision, relayed by the Contest Planner (U-195, Epic 39); the owner chose it as "step 2", then as an epic of its own: "I want Heartbeat enhancements, persona and memory enhancements to be able to be on different lanes so they can ship quickly."; and, approving it: "Approved, but on the heartbeat, I want to make sure that the model isn't being called every minute, It should only be called every 30 minutes and when a decision needs to be made."]. A person's scheduled check-in (PRD FR-63 to FR-66) runs on the same one-minute `Tick` through `Checks`. It adds no scheduler, task or timer.
    - **The model and the tick.** The tick never calls the model: it only finds the beats that are due. A beat makes at most one model request (one Triage decision, plus one read-only turn on act), and a person's beats fall due at most once per interval.
    - **Values.** A person's heartbeat values are per-person settings (AD-19): on, interval, active hours and checklist. The interval is 30 minutes by default and never less (30, 60, 120, 240, 480 or 1440). `Checks` reads the values on each tick through `OcuTalon.Data.Setting.Get`.
    - **State.** `OcuTalon.Data.HeartbeatBeat` (Lane A) keeps one row per session that has had a beat.
      - It holds `SessionRef`, `NextDueAt`, `LastBeatAt`, `LastItemId`, `LastOutcome`, `LastFallback`, `LastCostUSD`, `LastDecisionRef`, `LastTraceSessionId`, `LastSkipAt`, `LastSkipReason`, `LastRunNowAt`, `BackoffUntil`, `LastSnapshotKey` and `LastQuietKey`.
      - Nothing that decides "due" lives in memory (AD-5), and a beat missed during a restart runs once, as a check does.
    - **A due beat.** On each tick, after the due checks and in its own Try/Catch, `OcuTalon.Checks.Beat.RunDue(tickAt)` takes every person whose heartbeat is on and whose `NextDueAt` has passed.
      - **When it skips:** it makes no model request, records the skip reason and moves `NextDueAt` on when any of these holds, checked in this order:
        - the checklist is empty;
        - the tick is outside the person's active hours, in the instance zone through `Data.Time`;
        - `BackoffUntil` hasn't passed;
        - the day's cost cap is reached, read through `Bridge.Budget.State()`;
        - no model key is set, read through `Bridge.Keys.Status`;
        - the snapshot is unchanged since the last beat whose outcome was Ignore: `Checks.Beat.Snapshot` builds it by code, and its key (`Checks.Beat.Key`, a SHA-256 of the checklist and the snapshot's entry lines, its time line left out) equals `LastQuietKey`.
      - **Otherwise it builds one `OcuTalon.Msg.Item`:**
        - provenance `untrusted` and channel `heartbeat`;
        - the person's `SessionRef`, and Subject "Heartbeat check-in";
        - Text holding the checklist and that snapshot. The snapshot covers open incidents, watches with their last check result, pending approval requests, reminders due before the next beat, and the decisions since the last beat that made a decision, other than beats. It is at most 4,000 characters, and holds no diagnosis cause, no decision reasons and no event-log or message text.
      - **Where the Item goes:** asynchronously to `Intake`, in a trace session of its own whose `SuperSession` is the session's `TraceRef` (AD-26). So the beat, its decision and its cost are in Visual Trace like any untrusted item (AD-30). The row records the snapshot's key as `LastSnapshotKey`.
    - **Run now.** `OcuTalon.Checks.Msg.BeatNow` comes from `CommandsIn`, for verb `RunHeartbeat`, origin `web_user` only. It is the person's explicit request: it runs one beat at once, and active hours and an unchanged snapshot don't hold it back, while the other skip rules do. It runs at most once every 2 minutes per session, and inside the daily cap.
    - **Names** [ADDED 2026-11-03, Story 39.1, as its acceptance criteria list them]. Lane B's Story 39.2 creates, in `OcuTalon.Msg.Command`, the verbs `SetHeartbeat` (`On`, `Interval`, `From`, `To` and `Checklist`, plus the common command fields `Origin`, `IdempotencyKey` and `SessionRef`), which `CommandsIn` routes to `SettingsOps`, and `RunHeartbeat`, which it routes to `Checks` as a `BeatNow`, both from origin `web_user` only; and it appends the channel value `heartbeat` at the end of `Msg.Enum` CHANNEL. The per-person keys are `person.<SessionRef>.heartbeat.on`, `person.<SessionRef>.heartbeat.interval`, `person.<SessionRef>.heartbeat.from`, `person.<SessionRef>.heartbeat.to` and `person.<SessionRef>.heartbeat.checklist`, with the defaults and caps of AD-19.
    - **Outcomes.** The beat gets one attention decision from Triage, which has no tools (AD-7).
      - Ignore records only and sends nothing. This is the silent beat.
      - Digest, Tell now and Act go as for any untrusted item. On Act, `Intake` runs a read-only untrusted turn, and any further action is an offer the person can ask for in their own turn (AD-10).
      - On the next tick, `Checks` reads the beat's `Decision` by `ItemId` and records its outcome, fallback flag, cost and trace session. When the outcome is Ignore, it copies `LastSnapshotKey` to `LastQuietKey`.
      - A fallback still notifies (AD-8), and it sets `BackoffUntil` 4 hours out (or one interval, if longer), so an outage sends a few notices a day at most.
    - **Tests pin the model calls.** `OcuTalon.Test.Checks` counts requests in the CI mock's log: none on a tick with no due beat, none on a due beat whose snapshot is unchanged since the last Ignore, and at most one on a due beat.
    - **No all-clear (AD-28).** A beat is not a background check.
      - It has no `BackgroundCheck` row, check result or coverage-list entry, and no part in an all-clear.
      - No code path renders a beat, or an Ignore, as "everything is fine".
      - Its reasons pass the guard like every model text.
      - Status answers in the chat still come only from `check_status` and `Checks.Render`.
    - **Event.** After each beat, skip or result, `Checks` publishes the `heartbeat` live event (`OcuTalon.Live.Payload.Heartbeat`, the row's public view) to the session's stream, except a tick's skip because the checklist is empty, which saves its row and publishes nothing; a run-now still publishes [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 39.7, U-283 (the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation, at=2026-11-05T07:37:07Z#857), orchestrator].
    - **The online demo.** Values are per session and go with sign-out and the reset. The periodic beat is off until the visitor turns it on, the card offers a sample checklist, the 30-minute floor holds as everywhere, run-now follows the 2-minute rule, and every beat counts against the daily cost cap.

### AD-19: Configuration and secrets have fixed homes

- **Binds:** FR-5, FR-17, FR-38, FR-39, FR-40, FR-44, FR-45, FR-47, NFR-11, NFR-15, CON-3
- **Prevents:** a key stored in IRIS; a setting that lives in two places; mock settings in the judges' install.
- **Rule:**
  - **Model keys and bot tokens** come only from environment variables (`.env`, never committed; `.env.example` lists the names) through `@{env:VAR}` or `$SYSTEM.Util.GetEnviron`, and are never stored in IRIS or shown. A key placeholder stays unresolved through the Config Store and is resolved only inside AI Hub's provider; neither the entry nor a saved AI session ever holds the key (S5). The online demo's IRIS holds only a dummy model key and a dummy bot token; the outbound gateway swaps in the real ones (AD-25).
  - **Endpoint overrides** come from fixed optional environment variables that only the CI override and the online demo's deployment set: `OCUTALON_MODEL_BASE_URL` (ending in `/v1/`, S3), `OCUTALON_EXTRA_CA_FILE` and `OCUTALON_TELEGRAM_API_BASE`. The installer builds the provider configuration from them; with a CA file, it writes the full `tls` block with both `accept_invalid_*` flags false, and never sets `ALLOW_DANGEROUS_TLS`. A custom `anthropic-beta` header in `http_config.custom_headers` replaces AI Hub's own, so any beta header OcuTalon ever adds carries AI Hub's value too (S2). Only when the explicit CI-only setting `OCUTALON_MOCK_KEY_HEADER=1` is on (an environment variable that only `ci/compose.ci.yml` sets) does the step also add `x-ocutalon-mockkey` (the agent class's `MOCKKEY`) to each runner's `custom_headers`, which is how the CI mock keys its default scripts; `OCUTALON_MODEL_BASE_URL` alone never adds it, and online-demo traffic never carries it [AMENDED 2026-10-20, Story 0.11; owner decision 5].
  - **Config Store entries** (S5) are instance-wide, so their names are product-specific, in the three-part form S5 tested: `AI.LLM.OcuTalon<Agent>`, for example `AI.LLM.OcuTalonTriage`.
    - One install step, Lane A's `OcuTalon.Install.Step.Provider`, is their only install-time writer, and it reads the endpoint overrides itself.
    - It calls `%ConfigStore.DescriptorManager.RebuildRegistry()` first, modifies an entry that already exists rather than creating it, and writes each entry before any runner setting that names it, because setting validation expands `@{config:}`.
    - At run time `SettingsOps` changes an entry only through one bridge method that merges fields, then restarts the affected runners.
    - Entries keep the default `%Admin_ConfigStore` read resource; only production jobs read them.
    - The installer refuses a second OcuTalon namespace on one instance, so two installs never share an entry.
  - **Bridge values** (timeout, token budget per decision, request-size limit, daily cost cap, second model, model per runner) are host settings on the bridge items; prices live in `OcuTalon.Bridge.Data.Price` (including the above-100,000-token tier and cache-write tiers).
  - **Admin-entered credentials** (step 4): mail credentials in IRIS Interoperability credentials and OAuth2 tokens in the IRIS OAuth2 client, which the email adapters read natively; MCP credentials in the IRIS Wallet, referenced from the server's Config Store entry with `secret://`. Webhook secrets are stored as salted hashes. Every secret field on the settings page is write-only, and any admin-entered value containing `@{` is rejected, so no setting can expand an environment variable.
    - **Step 4 details (Story 35.2):** a webhook's secret is kept only as its row's `SecretHash`: a random 16-byte salt and the SHA-256 of the salt followed by the secret's UTF-8 bytes, stored as salt and hash in base64url joined by a dot; the hook recomputes it with the row's salt and compares the two hashes over their whole length in constant time, and an unknown `Ref` is compared against a fixed dummy hash so that it takes the same time. The creation and replacement routes keep the secret out of every command and message by the handoff of AD-1: the REST process puts the secret into a `SecretHandoff` event resource and submits `CreateWebhook` or `SetWebhookSecret` with only the handoff uri, `SettingsOps` takes the secret from it, validates it (16 to 256 printable ASCII characters from 0x21 to 0x7E, and no `@` followed by an opening brace) and writes the salted hash; the REST process deletes an untaken resource on every path. [AMENDED 2026-11-11, Story 35.3, S3 approved by the Contest Planner at the spec gate; the handoff rule itself is the shared one added under AD-1] For MCP servers, `SettingsOps.SaveMcpEntry(server, url, secret)` and `RemoveMcpEntry(server)` (stubs in Story 35.2, built in Story 21.4) write and remove the wallet secret and the `AI.MCP.OcuTalon_<server>` entry, called by the MCP settings routes before they submit `AddMcpServer` or after `RemoveMcpServer` (ruling planner-2026-11-09-35.1 Q4). The mail passwords never enter a command or message either; Story 21.2 fixes how they reach the Interoperability credentials. The mailbox's address and owning session are the product keys `email.address` and `email.owner` in `OcuTalon.Data.Setting`, written only by `SettingsOps` (Story 21.2) and read by `CommandsIn` (B1). [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 35.2]
    - **Handing a secret from the REST process to `SettingsOps`:** `OcuTalon.Core.SecretHandoff.Put(secret, .uri)` creates a uniquely named event resource and signals the secret into it; `Take(uri, .secret, timeoutSec)` reads the one message in the production job and deletes the resource, and a second `Take`, an unknown or malformed uri and an expired wait fail alike with no detail; `Discard(uri)` deletes an untaken resource. The resource lives in shared memory only: no global, command, production message, log line, trace or audit entry holds the secret. Stories 35.3 (webhook secrets) and 21.2 (the mail credential) use it. [ADDED 2026-11-11, Story 35-3-send-events-to-ocutalon-through-a-webhook, shared secret handoff]
    - **MCP settings writes [ADDED 2026-11-09, Epic 35 merge gate, Contest Planner ruling on DW-1041]:** the MCP settings routes never write the IRIS Wallet or the Config Store from the REST process (AD-1 is unchanged). Story 21.4 moves that write into `McpOps` (an operation). Until then the routes are stubs that change nothing (`Test.Core.CommandsStep4` `TestMcpEntryStubs`) and `Web.Api` has no `/v1/settings/mcp` route.
    - **Secret hand-off and the mail credential [ADDED 2026-11-11, Story 21-2-read-a-mailbox-and-send-email-over-pop3-and-smtp, shared secret handoff]:** a secret that a page sends reaches its writer through `OcuTalon.Core.SecretHandoff` (Lane B, Core). `Put` signals the secret into a uniquely named `$SYSTEM.Event` resource and answers `secret://handoff/<name>`; `Take` reads the one queued message and always deletes the resource, so a second `Take` of the same reference, an unknown reference and an expired one fail with the same plain status; nothing is stored, logged, traced or put in a command or message. The mail password is the one secret of Story 21.2: `SettingsOps` takes it and writes the Interoperability credential `OcuTalonMail` (user name and password) as the only writer. The server, the port, the `SSLConfig` (`OcuTalonClient`, server identity checked; there is no plain choice and no accept-any-certificate choice) and `Enabled` are System Default Settings rows of `EmailIn`, written only by `SettingsOps`, which has a detached job update the production (the update is not called inside a production job, where it leaves the job's registry unclean). The product keys `email.address` and `email.owner` are on `SettingsOps`' allow-list; the mailbox belongs to the session of the admin who saved last, and `CommandsIn` reads both keys from their rows. The job of `SettingsOps` runs as the Interoperability system user, which already holds what the two writes need (`%Ens_Credentials:W` for the password store and the write of the namespace's data database), so Story 21.2 adds no grant, role or resource. [ADDED 2026-11-11, Story 21.2]
    - **The outgoing mail server [ADDED 2026-11-11, Story 21.6]:** `EmailOut`'s System Default Settings rows (`SMTPServer`, `SMTPPort`, `Credentials` `OcuTalonMail`, `SSLConfig`, `SSLCheckServerIdentity` 1, `Enabled`) are written only by `SettingsOps` from the form's Outgoing mail server (SMTP) field, with the same credential as `EmailIn`; an empty outgoing server writes `Enabled` 0 and deletes the server rows. Every successful email save has the detached job update the production, also when no row changed (the update changes nothing when nothing differs and starts an enabled item that is not running), so an identical re-save after an update that never ran applies what was saved. The sender reads `OcuTalonMail` at each send and puts neither the user name nor the password in a message, trace, log, audit entry or GET.
    - **The mail OAuth2 sign-in [ADDED 2026-11-12, Story 21.3]:** an admin may sign the mailbox in with Google or Microsoft 365 instead of a password (the form's Sign-in method). The client secret reaches IRIS's `OAuth2.Client` `OcuTalonMail` only through `SecretHandoff` and `SettingsOps`, where `OcuTalon.Channel.Email.OAuth.Register` (one method, `Sys`, switches to `%SYS`, AD-3) writes it; it is in no key, command, message, log, trace, audit entry, global or GET. The tokens live only in IRIS's `OAuth2.AccessToken` (application and session `OcuTalonMail`); OcuTalon reads only whether the row holds an access token and no error, never a value, and never refreshes or revokes. The product keys are `email.signIn` (`google` or `microsoft`; absent for a password), `email.clientId` and `email.oauthOwned` (the comma list of what OcuTalon created or enabled in IRIS: `client`, `server`, `app`), and the status mark `emailSignIn` `{at, provider, signedIn}`; a provider keeps no mail password (the credential `OcuTalonMail` keeps the user name and an empty password), and saving Password removes the client, its tokens, the server definition OcuTalon made and, when OcuTalon enabled it and no other OAuth2 client remains, disables `/csp/sys/oauth2`. A client `OcuTalonMail`, or a server definition for the issuer with other endpoints, that OcuTalon did not create is never changed or deleted. The provider endpoints are fixed `https://` constants (no discovery, no revocation endpoint in the server definition, which IRIS would call when it deletes a token) used over `OcuTalonClient` with the host name checked, the scopes are the minimum for POP3 and SMTP, and IRIS's client sends PKCE (S256). Plain HTTP to the CI mock exists only behind the CI-only `OCUTALON_TEST_OAUTH_MOCK=1` (set only in `ci/compose.ci.yml`; a warning is logged whenever it is on). `/csp/sys/oauth2` is enabled only during an admin's register or sign-in action, and never again after an admin disabled it until the next explicit sign-in; every enable and disable is audited (`email.oauthApp`). Token use by the adapters, refresh and revocation are Story 21.8's.
  - **Structural or seed.** The installer-step registry marks every value it writes:
    - `structural` values are rewritten on every start: entry names, `base_url` and `tls` from the environment, the extension classes, the endpoint and adapter settings other than `Model`, the public settings and, on the online demo, `Decide`'s three budget settings;
    - `seed` values are written only when absent, and after that `SettingsOps` owns them: each runner's `Model` and every allow-listed bridge or product value.

    So a choice made on the settings page survives a restart and an upgrade. `SettingsOps` changes only the allow-list, and keeps the bridge timer at or under 110 seconds (AD-8).
  - **Product values that no bridge class reads** (digest time, thresholds, demo mode, the idle cap) live in `OcuTalon.Data.Setting` (demo mode is the seed key `demo.mode`, "1" or "0", which the `Demo` step seeds to "1" when `/opt/ocutalon/version` exists, that is, in OcuTalon's own image, and to "0" otherwise [AMENDED 2026-10-20, Story 0.10, Rule 22 ruling]), read through `OcuTalon.Data.Setting.Get` with a 60-second cache.
  - **Per-person values** [AMENDED 2026-10-31, owner decision, relayed by the Contest Planner (U-195, Epics 39 and 40); the decision record: the heartbeat "is stored with the settings (SettingsOps)" and "a persona belongs to a person"]. A value that belongs to one person, not the instance, lives in `OcuTalon.Data.Setting` under the key `person.<SessionRef>.<area>.<name>`.
    - **Areas:** `heartbeat` (`on`, `interval`, `from`, `to`, `checklist`) and `persona` (`preset`, `language`, `custom`). The heartbeat's defaults are `1`, `30`, `08:00`, `20:00` and "" (empty), and `on` is `0` on the online demo (AD-18) [ADDED 2026-11-03, Story 39.1].
    - **Whose values:** on a local install the session is the IRIS user's durable one, so values are per user. On the online demo they are per visitor's session, and gone at the reset.
    - **The writer.** `SettingsOps` stays their only run-time writer.
      - A person changes only their own values, through session-checked endpoints that submit with origin `web_user`.
      - The `SessionRef` comes from the server-side session (AD-4), never from the client.
      - An admin gets no right to another person's values.
      - Each change is audited (type `Settings`, AD-17) and applies within 60 seconds, as the cache expires.
    - **Validation.** Every value is refused when it holds `{{`, `@{` or a control character other than a line break; emoji and other printable characters are allowed. Each value also has the limit its FR sets:
      - checklist and custom persona: at most 1,000 characters, the length of `Setting.Value`;
      - language name: at most 40 characters of letters (any script), spaces, hyphens and parentheses, matched by `^\p{L}[\p{L} ()-]{0,39}$`;
      - interval: 30, 60, 120, 240, 480 or 1440 minutes, so nothing under 30 minutes on any install (U-195);
      - preset: a value from `Msg.Enum`'s fixed list;
      - active hours: `HH:MM` on the quarter hour, or all day;
      - on the online demo, the custom persona is refused.
    - **At sign-out.** `SessionOps.End` deletes the ended session's `person.<SessionRef>.*` keys.
    - **Proposed changes.** A persona the agent proposes is written only by `SettingsOps`, from an approved `setting` request (AD-11).
  - **Online-demo start values** come from fixed environment variables that only the online demo's deployment sets (AD-25), read on every start by the after-start script's `Public` step (structural values), which runs right after the bridge installer and before every step that reads them (AD-23). Without `OCUTALON_ONLINE_DEMO` the instance isn't the online demo.

    | Variable | Value (default) | Goes to |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `OCUTALON_ONLINE_DEMO` | `1` turns the online demo on | public key |
    | `OCUTALON_DEMO_USER`, `OCUTALON_DEMO_PASSWORD`, `OCUTALON_READONLY_USER`, `OCUTALON_READONLY_PASSWORD` | the published accounts (shown on the splash; not secrets) | public keys, and the users step |
    | `OCUTALON_RESET_INTERVAL_HOURS` | (4) | public key |
    | `OCUTALON_RESET_ANCHOR`, `OCUTALON_LAST_RESET` | UTC ISO 8601 | public keys |
    | `OCUTALON_LAST_RESET_KIND` | `scheduled` or `unscheduled` | public key |
    | `OCUTALON_PROXY_ADDRESS` | the proxy's address (AD-13's forwarded-address rule) | public key |
    | `OCUTALON_CONNECTION_CAP`, `OCUTALON_CONNECTION_RESERVE`, `OCUTALON_MESSAGE_ALLOWANCE` | (8, 2, 50 an hour) | public keys |
    | `OCUTALON_DAILY_COST_CAP_USD` | (20) | `Decide` host setting `DailyCostCapUSD` |
    | `OCUTALON_GATEWAY_SPEND_USD`, `OCUTALON_GATEWAY_SPEND_DATE` | today's gateway spend and the instance-zone date it counts | `Decide` host settings `GatewaySpendUSD`, `GatewaySpendDate` |

    **`Decide`'s budget host settings** are System Default Settings for item `Decide`, read by `OcuTalon.Bridge.Budget.State()` and `Decide`'s precheck through the bridge's settings accessor (AD-20): `DailyCostCapUSD` (0 means off), `GatewaySpendUSD` and `GatewaySpendDate` (an instance-zone date). On the online demo the `Public` step (Lane A) writes all three on every start (structural there), before `Production` starts, so the items start with them. On a local install `DailyCostCapUSD` is a seed value (0, off) that `SettingsOps` owns from step 2, and the two gateway values aren't written. The bridge never reads the public settings (AD-2).
  - **Public settings** live only in `^OcuTalonPublic` in `OCUTALON_PUBLIC` (AD-3). The keys are fixed: the online-demo flag, the demo and read-only user names and passwords, the reset interval, anchor, last reset and its kind, the proxy address, the connection cap and reserve (AD-13), the message allowance per session per hour (shown on the splash), and readiness. The accessor also computes the next reset.
    - One structural step, `OcuTalon.Install.Step.Public`, writes them on every start from the variables above, through the accessor, right after the bridge installer, so the steps that read the online-demo flag or the published accounts (users, web applications) find them; readiness stays cleared until the same step sets it last, after `Production` and `Reconcile` (AD-23). The after-start script clears readiness first, through the accessor too, when that class exists (nothing to clear on a fresh volume).
    - `SettingsOps` never writes them.
    - **`telegramIncluded`** [AMENDED 2026-11-02, Story 12.9, U-219]. The fixed keys also include `telegramIncluded`: `1` only when `OcuTalon.Channel.Telegram.In.Included()` returns 1 and a bot token is set on that start, otherwise `0`. `OcuTalon.Install.Step.Public` writes it on every start (a structural value), through the accessor, and only the online demo's splash reads it, so the splash and its banners mention Telegram only when this instance can really pair a chat. On the online demo that follows the host configuration: with no bot token in the IRIS container the key stays `0`, and once a token is set the key turns `1` at the next start with no code change. (The Contest Planner's answer to U-219, option (a'), under the owner's delegation, after the owner deferred Story 31.4 to after the v1.0.0 tag.)
    - `OcuTalon.Data.Public.Settings` is their only reader and writer, for every reader of a public value, the installer steps included, with no exception (`Get`; `Set` for the fixed keys only; `ClearReady`, `SetReady`, `NextReset`). Epic 0 implements it, not as a stub, because readiness is written from Epic 0; Lane B owns it after. `Setting.Get` delegates to it for those keys. A CI check fails if any other class references the global.
  - Installer steps write structural values and seed values; at run time `OcuTalon.Core.SettingsOps` is the only settings writer. It ships in release 1: there an admin switches demo mode with one documented terminal command, `do ##class(OcuTalon.Core.Admin).SetDemoMode(<0 or 1>)`, which requires the OcuTalon admin role or `%All` (a terminal caller holding `%All` can grant itself any role, so it is accepted too; every other caller is refused, the audit entry still names the person, and a change whose audit entry cannot be written is still refused; the web endpoints stay `OcuTalonAdmin` only) [AMENDED 2026-10-26, 4-6-simulate-incoming-email-in-the-simulated-mailbox, U-72, decided by the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation; it read "which requires the OcuTalon admin role"] and submits the change to `SettingsOps` through `Commands.Submit` with Origin `system` (FR-16). The settings page (step 2) adds every other value. It audits each change and applies it within 60 seconds: product values by cache expiry, bridge host settings as `OcuTalon.Bridge.Settings.Set` writes their row, read by `Decide` each time they are used for the live settings (`Bridge/Settings.cls:13-17`, `:144-170`; `Bridge/Process/Decide.cls:456-465`), so only the restart settings need `Ens.Director.UpdateProduction` or an item restart [AMENDED 2026-11-07, DW-773, as-built since 17.4, Planner; it read "bridge host settings by `Ens.Director.UpdateProduction`"], and model or provider changes by restarting the affected runner items (PRD FR-17 as amended on October 17). A Config Store edit takes effect only after that restart, which `SettingsOps` does itself; `UpdateProduction` alone doesn't apply it (S5).
  - **The bridge settings APIs** (Lane A; signatures in Story 33.1, built in Stories 17.3 and 17.4). `SettingsOps` changes bridge values only through `OcuTalon.Bridge.Settings`: `Providers()` (the provider table's rows in order, each with `keySet` from its key variable's presence and `supported`); `Values()` (`DecisionTokenBudget`, `DailyCostCapUSD`, `InstanceZone`, and per agent its provider and model and its second model's); `Check(name, value)` and `Set(name, value, *old)` for the allow-list `DecisionTokenBudget`, `DailyCostCapUSD` and `InstanceZone` (through `Bridge.Time.SetZone`); `CheckModel(agent, provider, model, second)` and `SetModel(agent, provider, model, second, *old)` for an agent's model or second model (a supported provider whose key is set, a model from the provider table; it merges the entry through `ConfigStore.Merge` with only the provider and `@{env:<its variable>}`, sets the runner's `Model`, for a second model also the first runner's `RetryItem`, an empty provider and model clearing it, and restarts the affected runner); `SetPrice(model, prices, *old)` (`Bridge.Data.Price`, non-negative); `TestRequest(agent, *request)` builds one request of the agent's own kind (`decide`, `diagnose` or `turn`), provenance `untrusted`, owner `instance`, a fresh `ItemId` and a fixed code-written input, which `SettingsOps` sends to `Decide` synchronously, and `TestAnswer(response, durationMs)` returns `{answered, seconds, reason}` (the reason a fallback reason). Every method refuses a value holding `@{` or off the allow-list with a plain-English reason and changes nothing; accepted values are seed values `SettingsOps` owns; `old` feeds `SettingsOps`' `Settings` audit entry; the bridge timer stays at or under 110 s. **Step 2 setting keys** in `OcuTalon.Data.Setting` (stored by Story 33.2, written only by `SettingsOps`, seeded by the digest area's install step in Story 17.5, read through `Setting.Get` with these defaults): `digest.time` (`HH:MM`, `08:00`), `digest.channels` (comma list of `web`, `phone`, `telegram`, default all three, each used only where the session has it), `checks.thresholds.diskFreePercent` (10), `checks.thresholds.journalFreePercent` (10), `checks.thresholds.licenseUsePercent` (90), `checks.thresholds.queueMessages` (100), `checks.thresholds.queueWaitMinutes` (10). [AMENDED 2026-11-03, Story 33.1, orchestrator unattended ruling, veto U-246]
  - **Saving a settings section** (Story 33.2). A save is one `OcuTalon.Msg.Command.SaveSettings` (`Slug`, `Changes` as a JSON object of field to value, `ActingUser`) submitted through `Commands.Submit` with origin `web_admin`; `SetDemoMode` keeps its own verb. `SettingsOps` reads the section from its `XData Sections` (each field, its key or bridge name, its kind and its default) and validates every value before anything is written. The order is: parse and validate every field; drop the unchanged values; for product keys, in one transaction, each changed value's `Settings` audit entry and then its row, with a rollback on any failure; for bridge values, every `Check` first, then each `Set` with its audit entry, and a later failure sets the earlier values back and audits that. A refused value changes nothing and writes no audit entry, and no change is left without an entry. The allow-list holds the step 2 keys `digest.time`, `digest.channels` and the five `checks.thresholds.*`. `SettingsOps.Values()` is the body of `GET /api/v1/settings`, read from the rows and never through `Setting.Get`'s cache. [AMENDED 2026-11-04, Story 33.2, orchestrator ruling (U-268)]

### AD-20: Usage, cost, budgets and allowances

- **Binds:** FR-2, FR-15, FR-40, FR-52, NFR-3, NFR-4, NFR-15
- **Prevents:** totals that don't add up; budgets enforced in some paths and not others; an over-limit message answered with a fallback.
- **Rule:**
  - **Decisions.** Every `Decide` call, except a busy refusal (AD-6), records one `Decision` row with its own `Ref` and `Kind` (`decide`, `diagnose`, `turn`, `tool`), unique on (`ItemId`, `Kind`). `Decide` allocates the `Ref` before sending and passes it in the request. `Decide` also writes the row's `TraceSessionId`, the trace session that holds its decision message (AD-26; Story 1.3).
  - **Usage.** The runner, not `Decide`, writes one `OcuTalon.Bridge.Data.Usage` row per agent turn (one `Chat` call) right after `Chat`, even when `Decide` has stopped waiting. The row holds `DecisionRef`, `ItemId`, `Kind`, `OwnerRef`, `RecordedAt` (UTC), agent, channel, model, model-request count, input, output, cache-read and cache-write tokens, the largest estimated request size, and cost. Compaction's summarizer request gets its own row, with agent `compaction` and `Estimated` true, because its tokens aren't reported (S18); every other row has `Estimated` false.
  - **Totals.** A `Turn` stores its two `DecisionRef`s (triage and turn) and never a total. The ownership API offers `TurnCost(TurnRef)` and `RunCost(SessionRef, since)`. Tokens come from the `GetStats()` delta around `Chat` (AD-6, AD-9); build 154 gives no per-request tokens safely (S4). Input tokens are `prompt_tokens + cache_read_tokens + cache_creation_tokens`, because `prompt_tokens` excludes cached input, and each tier is priced at its own rate (S2). AI Hub reports tokens but not cost. Decision and turn totals are sums of their rows, carried in plainly named fields: `InputTokens`, `OutputTokens`, `CacheReadTokens`, `CacheWriteTokens`, `CostUSD`, `Model`, `AgentStepCount`, `DurationMs`. The evaluation set uses `OwnerRef` `eval`, which no session total or daily cap counts.
  - **Before sending,** `Decide` checks the token budget per decision, the daily cost cap (`DailyCostCapUSD`, 0 meaning off, AD-19; the day ends at midnight in the instance zone, through `OcuTalon.Bridge.Time`) and the request size.
  - **Status reads.** `StatusMonitor` and `/state` read the cap's state only through `OcuTalon.Bridge.Budget.State()` (spent, cap, capped, resets at, in UTC), and key presence only through `OcuTalon.Bridge.Keys.Status(agent)`.
  - **The online demo.** A reset erases the `Usage` rows, so the outbound gateway also enforces the daily cost cap from the usage in each response (AD-25). `Budget.State()` adds the gateway's spend that the deployment passes at start (written into `Decide`'s `GatewaySpendUSD` and `GatewaySpendDate` host settings by the `Public` step before `Production` starts, AD-19), so IRIS's own precheck and banner engage first.
  - **The day's cap, once.** The first decision of the day that falls back with `daily_cap`, from `Decide`'s precheck or from the gateway's 403, makes the bridge mark the day as capped, once, under a lock, and fire the HostSink event `budget.capped` (AD-31). Because the bridge records a gateway 403 too, `Budget.State()` then reports the cap reached, so the banner shows and later decisions fall back before sending, whichever path got there first. With the online-demo flag set, `OcuTalon.Core.BridgeSink` sends the daily-cap message (AD-32) to each session with activity; locally, a daily cost cap (from step 2) shows its banner and sends no chat message. On the online demo, a session that first becomes active later gets it with its first `daily_cap` fallback; each session gets it once a day, recorded on its `Session` row. A session has activity when it is open and its user has sent a chat message, pressed Break the feed or answered a request since it began.
  - Request size inside AI Hub's tool loop is bounded by arithmetic. Before a turn, `Decide` estimates the first request (system prompt, tool schemas, history and the new input); above 40,000 tokens it first compacts the conversation. Chat's `max_iterations` is 4, the shared tool-result base caps all tool results of one model step at 10,000 tokens together (AD-10), and output is capped at 8,000. The fourth request is then at most 40,000 + 3 × 10,000 + 3 × 8,000 = 94,000 tokens. The in-loop size is estimated from callback data for the step record only; nothing aborts a request inside the loop (S4).
  - **Compaction (S2).** `Decide` flags a `turn` for compaction; the runner does it in its own job, outside any callback, before `Chat`.
    - **The defect:** `Session.Summarize` cuts history by message count and can split a tool call from its result, which leaves the session permanently invalid.
    - **The guarded path (confirmed by S18):** the runner calls `ForkAndSummarize(summarizerProvider, "", k, <compaction prompt>, 0)`, which leaves the original untouched. `Summarize` in place is never used.
      - **The cut:** `k` keeps the last N user turns whole (N is 1 by default), computed from `GetContext()` read outside callbacks. If nothing older remains, there is no compaction.
      - **The prompt** is an Epic 0 constant whose first line starts `OCUTALON-COMPACTION-V1`. It asks for bare JSON in AI Hub's fixed summary schema: `user_goals`, `constraints`, `decisions`, `facts_learned`, `tool_actions` (each with a required `tool`), `open_questions` and `next_steps`. Every tool result goes into `facts_learned` verbatim, with its tool name, call id, arguments and values. AI Hub's default prompt dropped tool results, and any other reply shape makes compaction throw.
      - **The switch:** the runner switches the turn to the fork only after a check passes (the first non-system message is a user message, and every tool result follows its tool call); the conversation link moves to the fork only when `Decide` accepts the turn's reply (AD-8, "One arbiter moves the link") [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 17.8, DW-729/DW-730 rulings by the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation]. Then the runner `%Save`s the fork. The check caught an unsafe cut before any request was sent (S18).
    - **The fallback:** any exception from `ForkAndSummarize`, or a failed check, uses a fresh AI session seeded with a code-written summary of the stored user and assistant texts.
    - **The summarizer provider** is the runner's provider settings plus `extra_config: {"output_config": {"effort": "low"}}`, the only way effort reaches the summarizer, with `request_timeout_secs` 20 and no retries. AI Hub fixes the summarizer's cap at 8,192, which is above AD-7's floor. `Decide` adds that 20-second bound to the timer of a turn it flags for compaction, staying at or under 110 seconds (AD-5).
    - **The stored form:** one system summary message, plus a system action-log message when tools ran, at the head of the history. Both are appended to the system prompt on every request, so the first request after compaction misses the prompt cache. They survive `%Save` and `OpenSession`, and a later compaction summarizes them again. AI sessions no link points to are purged by retention (AD-27).
    - **Usage:** the summarizer's tokens never appear in `GetStats()` (S18). So the `compaction` usage row is an estimate, marked `Estimated`: input is the prompt plus the flattened transcript of the summarized messages, output is the stored summary's length, both at about 4 characters a token, priced at the summarizer model's rate.
  - **Thinking history** needs no fallback for Claude 5.5's rule: AI Hub never sends thinking blocks back, so resumed, summarized and tool-changed conversations all pass, even with the rule forced on (S2). A live smoke test in every release-candidate run repeats that check: three turns across jobs, a compaction through the guarded path and a tool change, with the rule forced.
  - The online demo's per-session allowance (50 messages an hour, rolling, counting chat, Telegram and guide prompts) is enforced by `OcuTalon.Core.Allowance.TryConsume(SessionRef, Channel, Text)` (Lane B, Story 2.4), under a per-session lock, which `WebChatIn` and `TelegramIn` call synchronously before they queue a turn. It returns allowed, `retryAt` and a first-refusal flag, and on a refusal it records the refused message itself as a `Turn` with no decision. `Conversation` never checks it again. On a refusal the channel service queues nothing, `POST /messages` returns 429 with `retryAt`, and the channel service whose call was refused (`WebChatIn` or `TelegramIn`) sends the one-time allowance message, a `Notification` of kind `allowance` (web chat and paired chat only, AD-14), on the first refusal in the window. Break the feed, Approve and Reject keep working.
  - **Partner notices and the mail allowance** [ADDED 2026-11-10, Story 23.5, Contest Planner ruling]. `MailAllowed` counts sent partner notices with the incoming simulated emails toward the online demo's 20 simulated emails an hour.
  - **Memory searches count too** (Story 41.13, DW-1031): on the online demo `CommandsIn` counts each `SearchMemory` against the same per-session allowance (the same `Limit()` and rolling window) through `OcuTalon.Core.Allowance.TryConsumeRead(SessionRef, .retryAt)`, once per submitted search and before anything is sent to `MemoryOps`. A refused search records no `Turn` and opens no allowance window or notice (so the chat's own first refusal keeps its one notice); `CommandsIn` answers `Outcome` `allowance` with `Data` `{retryAt, limit}`, which the web layer serves as `429` `allowance` with the messages route's body. A busy or failed search still counts and is not refunded. The list (a blank `q`) and the pinned read never count. With the flag off nothing is counted. [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.13]

### AD-21: Memory, documents and MCP reuse AI Hub where it reaches

- **Binds:** FR-41 to FR-43, FR-47 to FR-51, N8, N15
- **Prevents:** a second vector store for documents; memory without hybrid search; an MCP call outside governance; one session's documents in another's search.
- **Rule:**
  - **Documents (step 3)** use `%AI.RAG.KnowledgeBase` over `%AI.RAG.VectorStore.IRIS` with FastEmbed [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.4, owner ruling via the Contest Planner: "FastEmbed" is the FastEmbed model, run through the embedding worker: `OcuTalon.Docs.Embedding` (a `%AI.RAG.Embedding` subclass, `ModelName` `AllMiniLML6V2`, 384 values) calls `OcuTalon.Memory.Embedding.Embed` over Story 19.2's worker, within 1.3e-7 of AI Hub's own FastEmbed; AI Hub's `FastEmbed.Create()` and `%SYS.Python` never run in `DocumentOps`, in the Chat agent's job or in a callback (AD-6)]. Citations come from `source` and `chunk_index`. The search tool takes no filter, so on the online demo each session gets its own knowledge base and table, gone after the reset (S14):
    - **Creating:** `OcuTalon.Docs` creates each table (`OcuTalonKB.S<session>` and its `_Config`, or `OcuTalonKB.Docs` on a local install) itself, with AI Hub's exact columns and `DEFAULTGLOBAL` under `^OcuTalonP.`, and only then builds the vector store. AI Hub's own DDL would put the globals in the data database, and building on a missing table re-creates it there, so a dropped table is never reopened.
    - **Attaching per turn:** the fourth bridge extension class, `TurnToolsClass`, returns the session's knowledge base or none. The adapter then builds a fresh tool manager for the turn (the agent's tools, that knowledge base, the gate and the audit policy) and assigns it before `OpenSession`, in the same method that composes the session config (AD-6). A long-lived manager keeps the first knowledge base bound, so one visitor would search another's documents. [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.5, measured in `evidence/19-5-task0-turn-tools.md`: the composition is as built. `SessionConfigFor` asks the class of `TurnToolsClass` once for each request of kind `turn`, `decide` or `diagnose` (never `tool`), in the runner's job and outside every callback, before `CreateSession` or `ResumeSession`; a compaction's second and third config reuse the first answer. A class that returns no object leaves the agent's own tool manager in place, so no request adds a tool to it, an agent's skills keep working and the standalone bridge runs as before. When it returns objects, the adapter makes a new `%AI.ToolMgr` with the agent's toolsets, the tools that `OnAfterAgentInit` attached through `Attach` (recorded as specs), each object's `AddToManager` (a knowledge base's holder answers it) and the gate and the step audit policy, assigns it to the agent, keeps the agent's own manager and the objects referenced, and puts the agent's own manager back when `Answer` ends on any path. An agent that declares skills with such a request fails with an error that names the agent (`UseSkill` keeps its catalog on the agent). Measured on build 154: a session created or resumed after the assignment offers the new manager's tools and the gate decides their calls; a manager put back takes effect on the next request; composing costs about 0.1 ms and building the knowledge base about 60 ms; one job serves owners A, B, A with each search reading only its own table; dropping a table after a knowledge base was built over it leaves no class, table or global, and a search through that knowledge base re-creates nothing.]
    - **The tool:** its name is the knowledge base's `Name`, fixed as `documents`, with `TopK` at most 5 (FR-42).
    - **Indexing and search (Story 19.4):** [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.4, owner ruling via the Contest Planner, measured in `evidence/19-4-task0-documents.md`]. `DocumentOps` never calls `%AI.RAG.KnowledgeBase.AddDocument`, `AddDocuments` or `ReindexDocument` (a repeated add replaces every chunk of a `source`, and AI Hub's chunker neither bounds nor sections passages). It keeps the text in `Data.Document`, splits it with `OcuTalon.Docs.Passages.Split` (a Markdown `## ` line opens a section, a passage holds at most 1,000 characters, the line `\## ` is the escaped literal for Story 19.7), embeds each passage through `Docs.Embedding` and inserts one row for each into `OcuTalonKB.Docs` by SQL (`id` `<Ref>:<n>`, `source` the `Ref`, `metadata` `{source, chunk_index, document, section}`), eight passages to a message, sending itself `OcuTalon.Docs.Msg.Index` for the next batch (a self-message is handled after the current message, in FIFO order with the commands); `Data.Document.Sections` keeps the section of each `chunk_index`. The Chat agent attaches the knowledge base in its own `%OnInit`, in the runner's job and outside every callback (`OcuTalon.Docs.Store.Attach`; none on the online demo until Story 19.5 replaces it with `TurnToolsClass`). Measured: the audit policy receives the result of a search as the array `[{id, score, text, metadata}]`; a search of unrelated words still returns every passage with scores near 0; the best passage of an unrelated question scored at most 0.1132 and the best passage of a question the runbook answers at least 0.5671, so a passage is a citation when its score is at least `CITEFLOOR` 0.4 (`OcuTalon.Bridge.Citations`), and a reply that says "None of your documents cover that." carries no citation while the turn stays tainted because the search ran. [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.5: the attach in the Chat agent's `%OnInit` is replaced by `OcuTalon.Docs.TurnTools`, named in the `TurnToolsClass` item setting of `Decide` in `OcuTalon.Production`; `OcuTalon.Docs.Store.Attach` and the agent's `Documents` property are gone, and the tool `documents` is no tool of the agent's own tool manager (its inventory) but is offered to every local turn and to every demo turn of a session that has a table. `DocumentOps` writes each document's rows into the table its row names (`KnowledgeBase`), under the owner's shared lock `^OcuTalon.Docs(<owner>)`.]
    - **Removal:** the reset restores the golden copy, which holds no session tables, so every demo session's tables are gone with it; nothing drops them at the reset. A session ended by sign-out has its documents and memories dropped by `SessionOps.End` (Lane B) through `OcuTalon.Docs.DropSession(sessionRef)` and `OcuTalon.Memory.DropSession(sessionRef)` (Lane A, built in Story 19.5). [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.5: `OcuTalon.Docs.DropSession(sessionRef)` takes the owner's exclusive lock (5 seconds; a timeout is an error status and the sign-out stays resumable), deletes the `Data.Document` rows whose `OwnerRef` is the SessionRef exactly and drops `OcuTalonKB.S<id>` and its `_Config` table with `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS`, which leaves no class and no global; `DropSession` first marks the owner as being dropped (`^IRIS.Temp.OcuTalonDocsDrop`), so a batch of `DocumentOps` that holds the shared lock stops between two passages and the drop waits for a passage and not for a batch; the next batch finds no row and stops, and no table is made again. `OcuTalon.Memory.DropSession(sessionRef)` takes `MemoryOps`' lock `^OcuTalon.Memory(sessionRef)` and deletes the session's `Data.Memory` rows. Both are idempotent and publish no event, and both run in `SessionOps`' job.]
    - **Names:** `DocumentOps` (`OcuTalon.Docs.Ops`, Lane A) answers `AddDocument` and `RemoveDocument` from `CommandsIn` synchronously with a `CommandResult`, keeps each document in `OcuTalon.Data.Document` (`Ref`, `OwnerRef`: the session's `SessionRef` on the online demo, `instance` on a local install; `AddedBy`, `Name`, `ContentType` `markdown`, `text`, `pdf` or `docx`, `Size` in bytes, `AddedAt`, `State` `indexing`, `ready` or `failed`, `Reason` a code, `KnowledgeBase` the table, `Text` the stored text; Story 19.4 appends the passage-to-section map), indexes asynchronously (how is Story 19.4's) and is the single publisher of the `document` event. It accepts Markdown, plain text, PDF and Word (.docx), PDF and Word up to 10 MB (FR-54); a refused file gets a row in state `failed` with its reason code, so the list shows it. A chunk's `source` is the document's `Ref`. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1] An evaluation run's documents (owner `eval`: `OwnerRef` and `AddedBy` the literal `eval`, written by the class method `AddEval` of `OcuTalon.Docs.Ops` in `EvalIn`'s own process for a documents case, through the same batch step as `DocumentOps`' indexing, and removed by `RemoveEval` when the run ends) are listed by no route, publish no `document` event and send no message; `RemoveEval` refuses a document of any other owner [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.4 rework, lead ruling (comply with AD-22, AD-1, AD-33)]. [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.5: an evaluation run's documents are written to `OcuTalonKB.Eval`, which no person's turn searches (DW-901), and `EvalIn`'s `Start` first removes every document of the owner `eval`, with its passages, through `RemoveEval` (DW-900): a run starts by sweeping what a killed run left, and evaluation runs do not overlap. `OcuTalon.Docs.Store` owns the owner rule: `OwnerFor(sessionRef)` is the session on the online demo and `instance` on a local install, and `TableFor(owner)` gives the table.]
    - **Tables:** `OcuTalonKB.Docs` and `OcuTalonKB.Docs_Config` on a local install; `OcuTalonKB.S<id>` and `_Config` per online-demo session, `<id>` the session's `OcuTalon.Data.Session` row id (a SessionRef holds `-`, which an SQL identifier refuses); globals `^OcuTalonP.KB<suffix>` and `^OcuTalonP.KB<suffix>Config`, created by `OcuTalon.Docs` before AI Hub's `Build()`, whose `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` then does nothing. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1] [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.5: `OcuTalonKB.Eval` and `OcuTalonKB.Eval_Config`, with the globals `^OcuTalonP.KBEval` and `^OcuTalonP.KBEvalConfig`, hold the evaluation documents; `Docs.Store.TableFor` maps the owner `instance` to `OcuTalonKB.Docs`, `eval` to `OcuTalonKB.Eval` and a session to `OcuTalonKB.S<id>`, `<id>` the row whose `Ref` equals the SessionRef exactly; a table name whose suffix is not `Docs`, `Eval` or `S` and digits is refused before any SQL. A session's table is made by its first `AddDocument` (`DocumentOps`) and by nothing else, a fresh online-demo install holds none, and so does the golden copy.]
    - **The `document` event:** payload `OcuTalon.Live.Payload.Document`: `ref`, `name`, `addedAt`, `size`, `state`, `reason`, and `removed` true on a removed document's last event; to the owning session's stream on the online demo and the instance stream on a local install. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **Adding and removing (Story 34.2):** `OcuTalon.Web.Documents`, forwarded from `Web.Api`'s URL map (`<Map Prefix="/v1/documents">`), lists through `Ownership.Documents`, takes `{name, content}` (the file's bytes in base64, read from the request stream, never through a string), maps the extension (`.md` and `.markdown`, `.txt`, `.pdf`, `.docx`, in any case) to `ContentType`, refuses `wrong_type`, `too_large` (any type over 10,485,760 bytes) and `empty` with 400 `bad_document` before any command and writes no row (the page shows that row until it reloads), and submits `AddDocument`/`RemoveDocument` as `web_user`; a body over 13,985,112 bytes (the limit in base64 and the JSON around it) is refused with 413 `body_too_large` before it is parsed, and while `DocumentOps` is not running both routes answer 501 `not_built` without submitting. `DocumentOps`' failed rows use `unreadable`, `not_built` (PDF and Word until Story 19.6) or `failed`; the page's text for each code is the web app's. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.2, DW-722] The bound is declared twice in `XData BodyBounds`, each at `Web.Documents`' `BODYMAX`: in `Web.Api` for `POST` `/v1/documents` (the first dispatch) and in `Web.Documents` for `POST` `""` (the forwarded dispatch checks again with the path after the Map's prefix); `Test.Web.DocumentsApi` pins both equal to `BODYMAX`. [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.2 rework 2, DW-741] [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.7, owner ruling via the Contest Planner (the Q1 grant): a PDF or Word file that `Docs.Extract` cannot read is also a failed row whose `Reason` is the extraction's code (`encrypted`, `damaged`, `no_text`, `legacy_doc`, `contents_too_large`, `timed_out`, `wrong_type` or `failed`), and `not_built` no longer applies to them; the Documents page shows the six codes that the web app did not know before with the Planner's texts (`REASON_TEXTS`).]
    - **Uploads (Story 34.2 rework 1):** a message body holds no global stream (DW-448's key proof), so `AddDocument` carries `UploadRef`, the `Ref` of an `OcuTalon.Data.Upload` row (Lane B, `^OcuTalonP.Upload`: `Ref` and `SessionRef` compared exactly, `Content` a global binary stream, `CreatedAt`), and not the file's bytes. `Web.Documents` stores the row only after its not-running guard has passed and immediately before it submits, and deletes it when the submit fails or the owner does not answer Ok. `Upload.Take(ref)` returns the bytes as a temporary stream and deletes the row, and returns nothing for an unknown or already taken `Ref`; its consumer is `DocumentOps` (Story 19.4), which takes the upload when it handles the `AddDocument` that names it. `Upload.DropSession(sessionRef)` deletes a session's uploads, and the documents-and-memories drop step of `SessionOps.End` calls it beside `OcuTalon.Docs.DropSession` and `OcuTalon.Memory.DropSession`. [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.2 rework 1, U-287] A refused add (type, emptiness, a malformed body, size, the body bound) writes no row, because every refusal comes before the row. `Upload.Take(ref, sessionRef)` and `Upload.Drop(ref, sessionRef)` find the row through `OcuTalon.Data.Ownership.Upload`, so an upload of another session answers like a missing one. `Upload.Purge` deletes the rows that nobody took and that are more than one hour old, whatever `daysToKeep` says, without SQL (the routes run as the signed-in user): the Web area's `Purge` runs it, and so does each call of the documents routes. [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.3]
    - **PDF and Word (Story 19.6):** `OcuTalon.Docs.Extract` runs `src/ocutalon/python/ocutalon_extract.py` (pdfplumber 0.11.10 for PDF, python-docx 1.1.2 for .docx) in its own `%SYSTEM.WorkMgr` category, `OcuTalon.Docs.Extractor` (one worker), never in `DocumentOps`' own process (AD-6); the categories are created by the area's install step through `Config.WorkQueues`. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1] [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.6: `OcuTalon.Docs.Extract` is the caller side and holds no Python; it writes the bytes to a private temporary file below `<temp>/ocutalon-extract/` (deleted before `Read` returns, files over an hour old purged by each call that writes a file), queues one work item to `OcuTalon.Docs.Extractor` (the worker class and the category, one worker, on the shared base `OcuTalon.Memory.Worker`, which `OcuTalon.Memory.Embedder` also extends) and waits at most `TIMEOUT` (120 s); on a timeout the busy worker is stopped (`Clear`) and the next read is served normally (measured in `evidence/19-6-task0-extraction.md`). `Read` never throws and stores nothing: the answer is `{ok:1, text, cut, markers}` (markers `page` for a PDF, one `## Page n` line per page with text, numbered by its real page; `heading` for a .docx, `#` for Title and Heading n as n+1 `#` at most 6, so Story 19.4's `##` splitter gives PDF pages and Word Heading 1 sections; table rows with cells joined by ` | `) or `{ok:0, reason}` with the fixed machine codes `too_large`, `contents_too_large`, `wrong_type`, `encrypted`, `damaged`, `no_text`, `legacy_doc`, `timed_out` and `failed` (their wording is `Docs.Extract` parameters, and `Failure(code)` fits each into "I couldn't read that file: <reason>."; `too_large` and `wrong_type` are the web catalog's reasons byte for byte, `too_large` naming the size when the caller passes it). Limits: input `MAXBYTES` 10,485,760 (pinned equal to `Web.Documents` `FILEMAX` by a test), text `MAXTEXT` 200,000 characters cut at the last line break with a cut note, and `MAXUNPACKED` 104,857,600 bytes declared by a .docx's zip entries. The signature of the bytes decides the parser, not the declared type.] [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 19.7: `DocumentOps`' Text seam (`Docs.Ops.Text`) sends `pdf` and `docx` bytes through `Docs.Extract.Read` in the Extractor worker (never its own process), waiting at most `EXTRACTWAIT` (45 s, the wait for the lock included, inside `CommandsIn`'s 120 s), and indexes the text like Markdown: `Docs.Passages` reads `pdf` and `docx` as Markdown, so a PDF is sectioned by its `## Page n` lines and a Word file by its Heading 1 lines, and a failure is a failed row whose `Reason` is the extraction's code. One read runs at a time on the instance: `Read` takes the lock `^OcuTalon.Docs.Extract` after its type and size checks (the wait counts within the timeout; a lock not got in time is `timed_out`), so AD-21's one worker holds for simultaneous callers (DW-827). While a PDF is read, every Flate decoding of pdfminer.six asks zlib for at most the remaining `MAXUNPACKED` bytes plus one, through a stand-in for the `zlib` module of `pdfminer.pdftypes` that is put back afterwards, and its LZW, RunLength and CCITT fax decoders count against the same budget; once the budget is spent every later decoding is refused, so a decompression bomb is `contents_too_large` within seconds (DW-828, DW-927, `evidence/19-7-task0-files.md`). A .docx is read in document order through paragraphs, tables and content controls (`w:sdt`, recursively), with the runs of hyperlinks, inline controls, tracked insertions, simple fields, smart tags and custom XML (deleted text skipped, DW-926) and a nested table's cells inside the outer cell's text; text boxes are not read, and rows wrapped in a content control stay out (DW-831). A line of a file's own text that starts with `## ` is written `\## ` (DW-832), so only the page lines and the Heading 1 lines open sections.]
  - **Memory (step 3)** has its own table, `OcuTalon.Data.Memory`: a `VECTOR(DOUBLE,384)` column with an HNSW index, plus an `%iFind` index. Results merge vector and keyword hits by reciprocal-rank fusion. AI Hub's vector table has no HNSW or full-text index.
    - **Names:** `MemoryOps` (`OcuTalon.Memory.Ops`, Lane A) answers `SaveMemory` and `ForgetMemory` (from Epic 41 also `PinMemory`, `UnpinMemory`) from `CommandsIn`; reads go through `OcuTalon.Memory.Search(sessionRef, query, limit, *rows)` and `.List(sessionRef, *rows)`. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **Tools:** `save_memory`, `search_memories`, `list_memories`, `forget_memory` (added to `OcuTalon.Tools.Names` by Story 19.3), and the documents tool `documents`. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **Table:** `Ref`, `SessionRef`, `Text` (at most 2,000 characters), `SavedAt`, `ItemId` and `Origin` of the turn that saved it, `Embedding` `VECTOR(DOUBLE,384)`; Story 19.3 appends the HNSW (cosine) and `%iFind` indexes and checks where their globals land. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **Ranking:** reciprocal-rank fusion, k = 60, over the 20 nearest by cosine and the 20 best keyword hits, equal weights, top 5, ties to the newer `SavedAt`; the HNSW check runs at 10,000 memories of one owner. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **Indexes and plan (measured in Story 19.3; `evidence/19-3-task0-memory-indexes.md`).** `EmbeddingIdx` is `%SQL.Index.HNSW(Distance = "Cosine")` with the defaults of `M` and `efConstruction`, and `TextIdx` is `%iFind.Index.Basic(IFINDSHAREDDATALOCATION = "INDEX", INDEXOPTION = 0, LANGUAGE = "en", LOWER = 1)` (exact words, case-folded). Every global of both indexes is under `^OcuTalonP.MemoryI(...)` in OCUTALON_PRIVATE and `^IRIS.KNNIndex` stays empty. The parameter `IFINDSHAREDDATALOCATION = "INDEX"` is needed: with iFind's default shared dictionary, the first write leaves empty directory entries for `^IRIS.IF.WordI` and `^IRIS.IF.WordRI` in OCUTALON_DATA although the data is in OCUTALON_PRIVATE, and `PrivateData` then fails every start. The recall query is `SELECT TOP 20 ... WHERE SessionRef = ? ORDER BY VECTOR_COSINE(Embedding, TO_VECTOR(?, DOUBLE, 384)) DESC` (`OcuTalon.Memory` `NEARESTSQL`); its plan reads `EmbeddingIdx` with the owner filter applied during the walk (ACORN-1) and returns only the owner's rows, an owner with 3 or 5 memories among 10,000 others gets all of them, and its top 20 matched a brute-force top 20 in 19 and 20 of 20. The planner chooses the index from the table's statistics: a table whose statistics were taken while it held a handful of rows stays on a scan of the owner's rows at 10,000, and a gather on a table of 200 rows or more (not at 100) keeps the index plan at every size measured up to 10,000. So `MemoryOps` gathers the statistics of the table (`OcuTalon.Memory.Tune`, about 0.05 to 0.45 s) at every 1,000th row id. Inserting 10,000 rows takes 49 s row by row (51 s with 500 more) and 48 s as `%NOINDEX` plus `%BuildIndices`; deleting them takes 50 s. [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.3]
    - **Search, repeat, trust and listing (Story 19.3).** The keyword query is the OR of the query's distinct runs of letters and digits (at most 32), each in quotes, so that an iFind operator or a punctuation mark in a question is inert; the word `or` in any case is written with a backslash, because build 154 refuses a quoted `"or"` with a syntax error. A save repeats when the session already holds a memory with the same `ItemId` (the idempotency key before its last colon) and exactly the same text: the answer is `saved` with the first row's `Ref` and no second row is written. A recall is trusted output when the `Origin` of every memory it returns is `tool` (the only origin that `CommandsIn` takes a `SaveMemory` from, and so a text from a user's untainted turn); when any returned memory has another origin, the tool puts the turn's `Tainted` at 1, so a later `save_memory` in that turn is denied. `OcuTalon.Memory.List(sessionRef, *rows, *total)` returns the newest 20 memories and the owner's count. `Search` returns at most 5. [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.3] A memory that `forget_memory` forgets is taken out of the turn's `HostData.memories` again (the member goes when none is left), so a reply never marks a memory that its turn deleted. [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.3 code review]
    - A reply marks the passages and memories it used as Conventions "Citations and memories" says. [ADDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1]
    - **The web layer's memory reads** (Story 41.9): the web layer reads the memory list through `Ownership.Rows` (the `memory` kinds: the newest 20 and the count), and searches through the `SearchMemory` read verb to `MemoryOps`, never through `Memory.List` or `Memory.Search` itself, because the signed-in user holds no SQL privilege and no embedding service (AD-13). `SearchMemory` searches only the command's own `SessionRef`, which the web layer sets from the CSP session (AD-4). [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.9]
    - **The Pinned tab** (Story 41.13): the web layer reads the session's pins through the `ListPinnedMemories` read verb to `MemoryOps`, which answers the pinned memories, newest pin first, and `used`, the tokens of the core-memory block: `used` is the ceiling of the length of `Memory.CoreBlock` divided by `Memory.CHARSPERTOKEN`, the same computation as the pin cap, so the card's figure and a refused pin never disagree; the page shows the number it is given and never computes it, and `Web` never calls `Memory` or `Agent.Input` (AD-13). [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.13]
  - **Memory that learns, core memory, daily notes and precedents** [AMENDED 2026-10-31, owner decision, relayed by the Contest Planner (Epic 41); approved by the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation (U-195); the owner chose all four, and the decision record: "Only a person's answers become precedents, never the agent's own decisions."].
    - **Told and learned** (Epic 41's second part).
      - `OcuTalon.Data.Memory` gains `Source` (`told` or `learned`), `Pinned` and `PinnedAt`. These are appended, under AD-5.
      - Code decides the source. A memory is `told` when the person's own text in that turn asks OcuTalon to remember; a fixed list of phrases in `OcuTalon.Tools` decides that. Every other memory is `learned`.
      - The Chat agent may save a fact on its own only in a turn with provenance `user` that no tool result has tainted. The gate already refuses saving in an untrusted or tainted turn (AD-10, Story 19.3). So no email, webhook, message body, document or MCP result can become a memory.
      - **Forgetting really forgets:** it deletes the row, its vector and its keyword entry and any pin at once, so search, the core memory and every later session start lose it. The current conversation's earlier messages aren't rewritten (U-195).
      - **Learned saves are announced (Story 41.15).** When `save_memory` answers `saved` and the stored row's `Source` is `learned` (read through `Ownership.Memory`), `MemoryTools` puts `{memoryRef, text}` into the HostData member `learned`: each memoryRef once, at most 3, and taken out again when the turn forgets it. A told, refused or failed save adds nothing. `Conversation` keeps a usable reply's entries on the `Turn` as `Learned` (JSON, at most 3, malformed entries dropped, absent when none) and on the `Outbound`. `chat.message` and the `/v1/state` chat items carry the entries to show as `learned` (`Live.Payload.Part.Learned`, `{memoryRef, fact}`). `Eval.Msg.TurnResult` keeps `HostData.learned`. [ADDED 2026-11-10, Story 41.15]
    - **At compaction** (optional; it needs a HostSink event that AD-31 doesn't have, and that amendment is decided at Epic 41's re-open (U-195); Stories 41.10 and 41.11 stay optional until then).
      - Compaction may cover a link that has had a tainted turn only if that turn rotated it, which AD-10 3a makes always true. After a guarded compaction (AD-20) of such a link, the runner would report the summary's `decisions`, and those `facts_learned` entries that carry no tool name or call id. Tool results never become memories.
      - `BridgeSink` would submit each entry as a save, with source `learned` and origin `system`, deduplicated against the owner's memories.
      - If the AD-31 amendment is declined, Stories 41.10 and 41.11 are dropped, and learned memory comes from the person's own turns only.
    - **Core memory.** A person's pinned memories, newest pin first. Only the person pins: OcuTalon never pins a memory on its own (U-195).
      - **Cap:** at most 1,000 tokens, estimated at 4 characters a token. The memory operation enforces this hard cap when a pin is added: a pin past the cap is refused, and nothing is unpinned to make room.
      - **Forgetting** a pinned memory unpins it and deletes it at once.
      - **Pinning** is `MemoryOps`' (`PinMemory` and `UnpinMemory`, from origin `web_user` only), under the session's lock. The cap is measured on the rendered core-memory block, heading and quotes included: `Memory.CoreBlock(sessionRef, candidate)` builds it from the session's pinned rows (`Memory.Pinned`; a row saved before Story 41.8 is not pinned) and `Agent.Input.CoreBlock` is the only place it is formatted. A pin that would make the block longer than `Memory` `COREMAX` (`PINCAPTOKENS` 1,000 × `CHARSPERTOKEN` 4 = 4,000 characters) is refused with `over_cap`. `Agent.Input` renders the same block after the persona block and before the notes, and never cuts it, so a pinned fact is never left out. [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.12]
    - **Daily notes.** `OcuTalon.Data.DailyNote.Build(sessionRef, day)` (Lane B, no storage) builds a person's note for one instance-zone day, on read, by code, with no model call.
      - **What it holds:**
        - the times, channels and outcomes of the day's decisions;
        - the openings of the person's own messages, at most 80 characters each;
        - each incident's production, state, counts and fix kind;
        - each approval request's kind, answer, channel and time;
        - each reminder's text and due time, and whether it fired, was late or was cancelled.
      - **Size:** at most 2,000 characters a day.
      - **What it never holds:** an item's sender, subject or body, a tool result, event-log text, or model-written text (a diagnosis's cause, a decision's reasons). So it carries no untrusted content into a person's turn, and AD-10 3a holds.
    - **Precedents.** `OcuTalon.Data.Precedent` (Lane B) holds a person's answers that later attention decisions may learn from:
      - a "This was noise" mark on a tell-now or act notice;
      - the approval or rejection of a request of kind `fix` or `tool_call`.

      **Its only writer is `PrecedentOps` (Lane B), which accepts only:**
      - a `MarkNoise` command with origin `web_user` (the person, on their own notice);
      - a `RecordPrecedent` message that `AnswerOps` sends after `Approval.Answer` returns `done`.
      - for the evaluation only, the seeding of `EvalIn` (AD-22), which runs only while `OCUTALON_EVAL=1`: `OcuTalon.Core.Msg.SeedPrecedent` and `OcuTalon.Core.Msg.RemoveSeededPrecedents`, accepted from `EvalIn` only and only while the `EvalIn` switch is on. Each seeded row is marked eval-seeded (owner `eval` and its run's `EvalRunRef`), and `EvalIn` removes the rows it seeded through `PrecedentOps` after its run, so `ForgetPrecedent` stays a person's verb (origin `web_user` only, AD-1). A product session never reads an eval-seeded row: `Precedent.Similar` returns them only when asked for owner `eval`, and the ownership reads, the Memory card and the routes return none; a test pins that a person's turn beside an eval run sees none. `PrecedentOps` also removes a person's rows on a `ForgetPrecedent` from origin `web_user` (AD-1) and a session's rows at sign-out (`SessionOps.End`). The Prevents line and the `web_user` and `AnswerOps` writers are unchanged. [AMENDED 2026-11-08, Story 41.7 (planned at 41.2), Contest Planner ruling under the owner's delegation; DW-920, DW-921]

      No decision, model reply, tool or arriving item writes a precedent.

      **Each row keeps:**
      - the owner and channel;
      - a match key that code computes with `Precedent.KeyFor(item)`: the sender's address for mail, the source for webhooks and polls, `production:<namespace>/<name>` for check findings and fixes, and `heartbeat` for beats;
      - the decision's code-written `Summary`, and the outcome then;
      - the answer, and when and where it was given.

      There are no vectors in this version. The rows are purged after 90 days through the area's `Purge(daysToKeep)` (AD-27).
    - **Where they reach a model.** Only `OcuTalon.Agent.Input` renders them, as AD-7's context blocks.
      - **At a session start, in a person's turn:** the core memory, and today's and yesterday's daily notes. A session start is the session's first turn of the day, a turn sent `fresh`, or the turn after a reply with `SessionRotated`; `Conversation` sets the `sessionStart` flag.
      - A turn counts toward "the first turn of the day" only when its request reached a model: an earlier turn that fell back counts only if its reply decision has a Usage row of the session (so a first turn that fell back before any model call leaves the next turn a session start), and today's note is built for the turn being answered, leaving out that turn and every later one, so the input never quotes the current message back. [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.12]
      - **In the `decide` request of an untrusted item the session owns:** precedents, at most 3 with the same match key. Never in a person's turn.
      - **How Triage treats precedents:** as examples of the person's past answers, never as rules. They can't change a classification, an approval, or what an item may lead to (AD-10).
    - **On the online demo** all four are per session and gone at the reset. Sign-out drops them through `SessionOps.End`, which calls `Memory.DropSession`, and through `PrecedentOps`.
  - **Memory vectors** come from the Python `fastembed` package (pinned 0.9.0; AI Hub's `FastEmbed.EmbedBatch` throws on build 154). It gives vectors identical to AI Hub's AllMiniLM-L6-v2 from the same model cache (S11).
    - It is wrapped in `OcuTalon.Memory.Embedding`, an `%Embedding.Interface` class, so `EMBEDDING()` and embedding columns work.
    - It always runs in a `%SYSTEM.WorkMgr` worker (`OcuTalon.Memory.Embedder`, its own category, one or two workers of about 220 MB each), never in the calling process (AD-6).
    - `%Embedding.SentenceTransformers` is rejected, because it needs torch.
    - Step 3 checks that memory queries use the HNSW index at volume.
  - **The FastEmbed model** (about 97 MB) is baked at image build into a root-owned, read-only folder named by `FASTEMBED_CACHE_DIR`. AI Hub's FastEmbed and the Python package both read it, offline on first use (S11). An IPM install downloads it once into `<mgr>/fastembed/` during the install. A package can't set the IRIS process's environment, so OcuTalon passes that folder explicitly: the Python package's `cache_dir`, and the variable set inside its own worker before first use. Step 3's first story confirms this path for AI Hub's FastEmbed. [AMENDED 2026-11-05, Story 34.1, Task 0, evidence `34-1-task0-fastembed-path.md`] Measured on a clean build 154 instance after an IPM install, outbound network blocked: with `FASTEMBED_CACHE_DIR` set to `<mgr>/fastembed/` inside a fresh IRIS job and inside a `%SYSTEM.WorkMgr` worker before first use, AI Hub's FastEmbed finds the model downloaded there and embeds offline; without the variable it does not find it. The variable must be set before `FastEmbed.Create()` in the job, not before the job's first `%AI` call. The download at install is an installer step of the Memory area (Story 19.2), made the same way. [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.2, Task 0, evidence `19-2-task0-fastembed-cache-dir.md`] Measured on a clean build 154 instance with outbound network blocked, in a `%SYSTEM.WorkMgr` worker: the Python package `fastembed` 0.9.0, given the folder as `cache_dir` (with `local_files_only=True`) and `FASTEMBED_CACHE_DIR` set to it inside the worker, loads the model offline from the image's read-only `/opt/fastembed` and from `<mgr>/fastembed/` as the Dockerfile's `snapshot_download` call made it, and returns 384 values; the model name `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` resolves to the folder `models--Qdrant--all-MiniLM-L6-v2-onnx`. It needs no `HF_HUB_OFFLINE`, writes nothing into the folder, loads in under a second once the files are cached, and the worker grows by about 200 MB. Its vectors differ from AI Hub's FastEmbed vectors by at most 1.3e-7 for an ASCII text, a text with an accented letter and CJK characters, and a text over 256 word pieces. Names: the class `OcuTalon.Memory.Embedder` holds the one rule for the folder, `ModelFolder()` (the process's `FASTEMBED_CACHE_DIR` when set and not empty, else `<mgr>/fastembed/`), and the worker entries; the category `OcuTalon.Memory.Embedder` allows 2 active workers (1 by default); the configuration row is `OcuTalonMemory` (`modelName` and `dimensions` 384, `VectorLength` 384); the install step is `OcuTalon.Install.Step.Memory` (AD-23), which creates the category and the row and downloads the model only when the folder lacks it, in a worker, and answers a download that cannot run with a warning, never a failure. The measurements above, and Story 34.1's with the folder made by hand, were on ARM64. On the x64 runner, both packages read the image's `/opt/fastembed` offline (and AI Hub's FastEmbed reads a copy of it under `<mgr>` from a worker): measured, `live:memory-offline` in `iris (shards-4)` printed `MEMORY-OFFLINE engine=x86_64 ok=1` (243 s, on Story 19.2's head) [AMENDED 2026-11-06, Story 19.2, CI measurement]; an embed from the `<mgr>/fastembed/` folder that the Memory step downloads on x64 stays inference, because the x64 `ipm-install` job checks that the step leaves the model there but embeds nothing from it. [AMENDED 2026-11-07, Story 19.8, DW-830] The hosts of the install's downloads, measured with HEAD requests only (`curl -sI`, redirects followed, no body fetched): the four small model files come from `huggingface.co` itself, and `model.onnx` is redirected to a download host under `hf.co` (`us.aws.cdn.hf.co` in the test installs) with a `Link` header that names `cas-server.xethub.hf.co` (Xet); PyPI adds only `pypi.org` (the index) and `files.pythonhosted.org` (all 72 wheel URLs of the 36 pins, for aarch64 and x86_64, without a redirect). The hosts that hf-xet 1.8.0 opens for chunk transfer are not visible to a HEAD request (inference). The README and `docs/install-ipm.md` name `pypi.org`, `files.pythonhosted.org`, `huggingface.co` and its download hosts under `hf.co`.
  - **MCP servers (step 4): AI Hub lists, OcuTalon calls (S9).** Servers are added only from the settings page, and never at run time on the online demo, whose installer preconfigures only the servers on the demo's allow-list (FR-47). Each is a Config Store entry `AI.MCP.OcuTalon_<server>` (its `url`, and any secret as a `secret://` wallet reference), plus an `OcuTalon.Mcp.Server` row and its IRIS resource.
    - **Listing:** the runner attaches each server in `OnAfterAgentInit`, after the agent's own toolsets, as `mcp:remote:@{config:AI.MCP.OcuTalon_<server>}`. It checks the attach status and the tool statuses. An unavailable server is reported, and an MCP tool whose name shadows a built-in is refused, with admins notified. The model sees the server's tools natively, so listing never taints a turn.
      - **Measured attach facts (Story 35.1, Task 0):** [AMENDED 2026-11-09, Story 35.1, Task 0, evidence `35-1-task0-mcp-attach.md`] On build 154, attaching through the Config Store placeholder works, and the server receives the header that the entry's `secret://` reference resolves to; the tool statuses and the gate see the placeholder spec, never the value. A server that is down makes `AddTool` return an error whose text holds the resolved spec, a credential value included, so the runner reads that text only to choose a reason word (`unreachable` or `failed`) and never logs, stores or reports it. A tool whose name is already on the manager is shadowed by AI Hub itself: it shows as `unavailable` in the tool statuses, is never listed and never runs, while the server's other tools stay, and the runner reports its name as refused. A server that goes down after the agent was built makes AI Hub's listing of a manager that holds it throw (measured in Story 35.1's code review; the text names the URL, not the credential), so every request of an agent with a server attached gets a manager of its own, to which a server that does not answer is not added.
