The tests' mock server binds the loopback only: `%IO.ServerSocket`'s `Open` with a timeout ignores the interface while the queue size is above 0 and keep-alive is not -1 (its defaults), so the mock sets keep-alive -1; with keep-alive -1 a queue size of 5 still binds the loopback only, and the mock keeps that backlog so a burst of connections under CI load is queued, not refused [AMENDED 2026-11-11, Story 22.2, Task 0 (DW-1038), measured on a lane-A warm throwaway: `OcuTalon.Test.Mcp.Loopback` stayed green with queue size 5 and keep-alive -1; it read "so the mock sets a queue size of 0 and keep-alive -1"].
    - **Transport:** the settings page accepts only `https://` URLs whose certificate the container's trust store accepts, or loopback for development. AI Hub refuses plain HTTP to any other host, and an untrusted certificate makes the tools silently unavailable.
    - **Calling:** AI Hub's MCP execution can't be cut (S9), so the gate rewrites every allowed MCP call into the governed proxy tool `call_mcp_tool(server, tool, arguments)`. That tool calls the server with OcuTalon's minimal Streamable HTTP client (`initialize`, `tools/call`) and cuts the result through `OcuTalon.Tools.Result` (AD-10). The gate classifies a direct call to the proxy by its (server, tool) arguments, the same way.
    - **Changes:** a tool the server adds after the runner's build is listed but can't be called until the runner item restarts, so `SettingsOps` restarts the runner when an admin approves new tools. After a server is added or removed, `McpOps` (Lane A) calls `OcuTalon.Core.SettingsOps.RestartRunners()` (Lane B, Story 35.2 (was 21.1)), which restarts the runner items through `Ens.Director`. A changed fingerprint makes a tool unclassified and notifies admins; removal cancels pending requests for its tools. Its tools arrive unclassified: every discovered tool has an `OcuTalon.Mcp.ToolClassification` row, unclassified included.
    - **The settings page's tool list** reads `OcuTalon.Mcp.Ops.Tools(server)` (Lane A; fixed by Story 35.1 (was 22.1), built in Story 22.2): each discovered tool's name, classification and status, from those rows.
    - **Names (Story 35.1):** the verbs `AddMcpServer`, `RemoveMcpServer`, `ClassifyTool`, `SetSourcePoll` and `RemoveSourcePoll` (classes in `OcuTalon.Msg.Command`, Story 35.2) come from origin `web_admin` only, are refused from `tool`, and are routed by `CommandsIn` to `McpOps`. `OcuTalon.Core.SettingsOps` writes the server's wallet secret and its `AI.MCP.OcuTalon_<server>` Config Store entry, called from Story 35.2's MCP settings route; `McpOps` writes only the `Mcp.Server` row and calls `RestartRunners()`. No command, log, test output or record ever carries a secret value, only a `secret://` reference (AD-19). `Mcp.Server` holds `Name`, `CreatedAt`, `AttachStatus`, `AttachReason`, `AttachedAt` and `RefusedTools`. The runner learns the servers and reports each attach (status, reason, refused tools) through `McpServersClass` (AD-31), `McpOps` records the report on the server's row, Settings shows it through `Tools(server)`, and Story 22.2's admin notices read it (the Listing bullet's "with admins notified"). [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 35.1, Contest Planner ruling planner-2026-11-09-35.1]
    - **`RestartRunners()` and the admins' sessions (Story 35.2):** `OcuTalon.Core.SettingsOps.RestartRunners() As %Status` restarts every runner item (class `OcuTalon.Bridge.Operation.AgentRunner`) that has a job in the running `OcuTalon.Production` through `OcuTalon.Bridge.Timeouts.Restart` (`Ens.Director.TempStopConfigItem` off and on, then a wait of at most 60 seconds in all for the items' new jobs), the production running throughout; with no OcuTalon production running it returns OK and restarts nothing, because the next start builds every agent with the servers registered then; an item that does not restart is the error. `OcuTalon.Data.Ownership.AdminSessions(Output sc) As %List` gives the Refs of the sessions whose user held `OcuTalonAdmin` (by its whole name, never `%All`) at their latest sign-in, from `Session.AdminAtSignIn`, which `OcuTalon.Web.SessionEvents` sets at each sign-in on a local install (an online-demo visitor's row keeps 0); it reads across owners with the privilege check and runs only in production jobs and tests; Story 22.2's admin notices read it. The verbs' properties: `AddMcpServer` (`Server`, `Url`, `ActingUser`), `RemoveMcpServer` (`Server`, `ActingUser`), `ClassifyTool` (`Server`, `Tool`, `Classification`, `ActingUser`), `SetSourcePoll` (`Server`, `Tool`, `Name`, `Arguments`, `IntervalMinutes`, `ActingUser`), `RemoveSourcePoll` (`SourceRef`, `ActingUser`); none carries a secret. [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 35.2, Contest Planner ruling planner-2026-11-09-35.1]
    - **`Tools(server)` (Story 35.1):** `Tools(server As %String, Output tools As %DynamicArray) As %Status`; each element has `name`, `classification` (`unclassified`, `read-only` or `risky`) and `status` (`available`, `unavailable` or `refused`). The built version (Story 22.2): an unknown server is an error, a tool's `status` is `unavailable` while the row's `AttachStatus` is `unavailable`, and `refused` when its name is in the row's `RefusedTools`. The stub (Story 35.1) gives `$$$OK` and an empty array. [AMENDED 2026-11-09, Story 35.1, extension of the Rule to the settings page's tool list]
    - **Step 4 build (Stories 22.2, 22.8, 22.9)** [ADDED 2026-11-11, Stories 22.2, 22.8 and 22.9 spec gates, Contest Planner ruling 2026-11-11 (U7 wording S1-S11 approved under its conditions)]:
      - **The per-server resource (S2).** `OcuTalon_MCP_<server>` (no public permission) and its `U` grant to `OcuTalonServiceRole` are created when `McpOps` handles an add, and removed with the server, only through one fixed entry point, `OcuTalon.Install.Step.Mcp.Grant(server)` and `Revoke(server)` (AD-3: only `Install.*` reaches `%SYS`). It can only create, grant or remove a resource named `OcuTalon_MCP_` followed by a valid server name, and a unit test shows it refuses any other name. It runs in `McpOps`' production job, whose privilege is the Interoperability system user's (AD-24's limits, unchanged): neither `OcuTalonServiceRole` nor `McpOps` gains `%Admin_Secure`, `%All` or any security-administration privilege for it. `Install.Step.Mcp.Grants()` declares the grants again at every start from the `Mcp.Server` rows, and declares none on an online-demo install, where `McpOps` refuses every MCP verb.
      - **Fingerprints at attach (S3).** The attach report carries `fingerprints` (tool name to `OcuTalon.Bridge.Context.ToolFingerprint(server, name, description, parameters)`, SHA-256 hex over a canonical, sorted-keys form of the server id, tool name, description and input schema; [AMENDED 2026-11-11, Story 22.8, recording the Contest Planner ruling on Story 22.4 condition 2(a): fingerprint form]). `McpOps.RecordAttach` keeps one `OcuTalon.Mcp.ToolClassification` row per discovered tool (refused ones included), deletes the rows of tools no longer listed, clears a classified tool whose fingerprint changed (`McpOps.ClearChanged`, audited), and sends the admin notices `mcp.serverUnavailable`, `mcp.toolShadowsBuiltIn` and `mcp.toolChanged` (kind `status`) once per transition. The gate side (S6, S7) is in AD-10.
      - **Bounded attach and listing (S8, S10).** Measured (Story 22.9, Task 0, evidence `22-9-task0-mcp-hang.md`): AI Hub gives no deadline (against a server that accepts and never answers, `AddTool` blocked 632 s, `%Discover()` 551 s and a built-in tool's `ExecuteTool` 300 s); `MCPDescriptor` takes no timeout key; every listing and every tool call lists each MCP server on the manager; `$SYSTEM.Process.Terminate` waits for the native call. So the agent's own manager holds no MCP server. The build attaches each server to a report-only manager after `McpServersClass.Probe` (OcuTalon: an unauthenticated JSON-RPC `initialize` with a 2 s limit, sent only to admin-registered servers and never on the online demo). Each request's manager adds only the servers whose probe answered. A failed probe reports the server `unavailable` (reason `unreachable`) and rests it 60 s per runner job; per-request reports go out on transitions. Agents that declare skills get no MCP tools.
      - **The governed proxy (S12)** [ADDED 2026-11-11, Story 22.3 spec gate, Contest Planner ruling (U7 wording S12-S14 approved under its conditions)]: `call_mcp_tool` is offered only by a request's composed manager that holds an MCP server, through `McpServersClass.ProxyToolSet()`; it is in no agent's `TOOLSETS`, never offered on the online demo, and refused there with `MCPDEMO` before any request. Its client extends `OcuTalon.Mcp.Client` (bounds 2 s `initialize`, 2 s `tools/list`, 20 s `tools/call`), verifies the server certificate (`VerifyPeer` on; plain `http://` only to this computer), and before `tools/call` re-lists the tool and refuses a changed fingerprint unrun. The `secret://` credential read at call time never appears in a message body, Visual Trace, log or audit entry. One `MCP` audit entry per run (`called` or `refused`) holds server, tool and outcome only. A result taints the turn and is cut to the request size limit before it reaches the model. The proxy never writes the classification row. If the gate cannot reroute a native MCP call, it denies it with `MCPUSEPROXY` and the model calls `call_mcp_tool` itself (fallback F1, pre-approved).
      - **The mid-request residual (S11).** A server that stops answering after its probe passed, within the same request, still holds that runner job until it closes the connection. Accepted for this release (ruling (a)): Story 22.6 documents it, and Story 22.10 amends this AD so that OcuTalon lists and offers MCP tools through its own bounded client.
  - **Source polls (step 4)** call `call_mcp_tool` through a `tool` request to `Decide`, so the gate, the audit policy and the agent step apply with no model.
  - **OcuTalon as an MCP server (step 7, after the contest (AD-29); confirmed by S10)** is `OcuTalon.Mcp.Service`, a `%AI.MCP.Service` subclass offering only read-only toolsets, with `Parameter RESOURCE = "OcuTalon_MCPServer"`.
    - **The web application:** the installer creates `/mcp/ocutalon` with password authentication only, namespace OCUTALON and no application roles.
    - **`iris-mcp-server`:** it runs with no endpoint credentials (each client reaches IRIS as its own user), `allow_anonymous = false` and its status tool off. Its bridge login is a role-less user whose password comes from the environment.
    - **Never configure endpoint credentials:** an endpoint user and password in its configuration let anonymous clients in whatever `allow_anonymous` says (S10).
    - **Clients** authenticate as IRIS users holding `OcuTalonMCPClient` (AD-24), and the tools run as that user.

### AD-22: Testing, CI and the evaluation set

- **Binds:** REL-1 to REL-5, FR-4, FR-38, FR-62, NFR-1, NFR-2, NFR-5, CON-1, CON-3
- **Prevents:** mocks leaking into the judges' install; one test's mock scenario breaking another's; an evaluation set that tests a different path from the product.
- **Rule:**
  - ObjectScript tests are `%UnitTest` classes under `OcuTalon.Test.<Area>`, run on the self-hosted IRIS runner, sharded by area, each shard in its own compose project. Web and mock code runs on hosted runners.
  - A CI-only compose override adds one mock service for the model API and the Telegram API, reached through the AD-19 endpoint overrides. The model path uses the Anthropic provider's `base_url` over plain HTTP on the compose network, with no private CA or host alias (S3: the product's own request path, so REL-4's same-path rule holds; the matching PRD note is P3, applied October 17).
  - **What the mock serves.** It answers `POST /v1/messages` and the model lookup `GET /v1/models/<id>` that AI Hub makes when an agent starts. It rejects sampling parameters and output caps under 4,000. Its scenarios cover the `refusal` and `max_tokens` stop reasons, an empty reply, a provider error, the provider's "Invalid input" rejection, an answer held past the bridge timer, and 429 and 529 with `Retry-After`.
  - **How a request picks its scenario:**
    - by a marker in the newest user message that has text (`[[mock:<name>]]`);
    - else by a guide prompt, when the user message contains that prompt's exact text (it doesn't start with it, because the `Agent.Input` preamble comes first; scenarios under `ci/mock/scenarios/B/`);
    - a guide-prompt scenario under `B/` may also name the mock key of the agent it answers (`match: {prompt, mockkey}`): it then answers only requests whose agent has that `MOCKKEY`, and any other request that reaches the same prompt falls through to the next rule; a `B/` scenario without `mockkey` answers every request whose message contains its prompt, as before. This is CI-mock-only: nothing a judge runs changes [AMENDED 2026-10-31, Story 11.7, U-194, decided by the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation];
    - else by the default script keyed by the agent class's `MOCKKEY` parameter, never by prompt wording. The tool names those scripts call are constants fixed in Epic 0.
    - The agents' `MOCKKEY` values are `triage`, `diagnosis` and `chat`; the 12 built-in tool names are defined once in `OcuTalon.Tools.Names`; the compaction prompt lives in `OcuTalon.Bridge.Compaction` and starts with `OCUTALON-COMPACTION-V1` [AMENDED 2026-10-19, Story 0.6, Rule 22 ruling] [AMENDED 2026-11-01, Story 5.13 merge, orchestrator ruling (veto U-199, kept by the owner's delegate): as-built count 11 to 12, `recent_activity` added].
  - **Summaries.** It recognises a summarizer request by `OCUTALON-COMPACTION-V1` at the start of its `system` text (AD-20), so markers quoted in a summarized transcript don't fire again. It answers in AI Hub's summary schema, with `facts_learned` and at least one `tool_actions` entry. One scenario answers off-schema, to exercise the fallback.
  - **Logs and state.** It logs header names and the key's length only. Its only state is a per-test countdown for retry scenarios, keyed by the test's own marker. One scenario sends a long `Retry-After` and asserts that the wait is capped at the retry block's back-off (S18). Scenario files live under `ci/mock/scenarios/<lane>/`, one JSON file per scenario (`<name>.json` with `name`; `match`, holding one of `marker`, `prompt`, `mockkey` or `telegram`, or `prompt` together with `mockkey` for a `B/` guide-prompt scenario that answers one agent only [AMENDED 2026-10-31, Story 11.7, U-194, decided by the Contest Planner under the owner's delegation]; and `replies`, each with `status`, `headers`, `delayMs`, `stopReason`, and `text`, `toolUse` or `body`). Lane A extends the mock only by adding files under `ci/mock/scenarios/A/`; new mock behaviour is Lane B's, asked for under AD-33.
  - **The Bot API side (Lane B, Story 10.3).** A scenario whose `match` is `telegram` names a Bot API method (such as `getUpdates` or `sendMessage`), and its `replies` use `status`, `headers`, `delayMs` and `body` (Bot API bodies, including errors such as `{"ok": false, "error_code": 409, ...}` and 429 with `parameters.retry_after`). The control endpoint queues inbound updates, queues replies per method, and returns every call it recorded (method and parameters). The token is logged by length only, including where it appears in the URL path (`/bot<token>/`). `getFile` and the file-download path arrive in Story 25.3 (Lane B), for Story 26.2.
  - CI tests include: the demo path (REL-2, a Playwright test of UJ-1's web route on every pull request and push to `main`), every fallback reason, the production starting with no model key, a risky MCP tool held for approval against a mock MCP server, the live ToolManager inventory, governance commands refused from origin `tool`, a cross-session dismiss and answer, the storage diff, the bridge boundary and `%AI` allow-list, and the INSTRUCTIONS `{{` check.
  - **The evaluation set** lives in `eval/` as recorded Items and recorded reader snapshots.
    - A run sends Items into `Intake` (or diagnoses into `IncidentManager`, or, for FR-37's all-clear cases, chat `turn` requests into `Decide` with recorded check results) through `EvalIn`, an adapterless service (Lane A) that the production keeps disabled. Only the environment variable `OCUTALON_EVAL=1` enables it, which Lane A's evaluation job sets (on every push and for each release candidate); Story 0.7's CI override passes `OCUTALON_EVAL` through from the job's environment (default 0). `EnsLib.Testing.Service` isn't used, because it needs the testing switch AD-16 keeps off.
    - Each run mints fresh `ItemId`s, keeping the recorded one as the Item's `SourceItemId` (AD-14), so it exercises the product path, `Agent.Input` and `EvidencePack.Build` included.
    - `eval` is a reserved owner (AD-2): `Intake` and `IncidentManager` record the decision and send an `OcuTalon.Msg.ItemDecision` with owner `eval` to the `EvalSink` operation (Lane A; AD-30), never to `OutcomeRouter` or `Notify`. For a `turn` case, `EvalIn` itself sends `EvalSink` an `OcuTalon.Eval.Msg.TurnResult` (Lane A: the case's `SourceItemId`, the reply's `DecisionRef` and `ToolsRun`, the filtered reply text, and the rendered catalog key and rows), after putting the case's recorded check results in the request's `HostData`, which `check_status` reads for owner `eval`. A test proves an evaluation run writes no `Notification`, `Delivery` or live event. It runs against the mock on every push and on Claude Haiku 5.5 for each release candidate. It includes the UX's suspicious-request email and an injected "stop watching" as injection cases. The recorded incidents span causes: no cause category accounts for more than 8 of the first 10, so a diagnosis that always gives the same answer can't reach SM-2.
    - **The digest case.** A case of kind `digest` holds recorded check results; `EvalIn` sends them to `Digest` as an `OcuTalon.Eval.Msg.DigestCase` (owner `eval`, `SourceItemId`, the recorded rows), `Digest` composes with those rows in place of `Checks.Coverage.Build` (its model request owner `eval`) and sends `EvalSink` an `OcuTalon.Eval.Msg.DigestResult` (`SourceItemId`, `CatalogKey`, `AllClear`, the named entries, `DecisionRef`) in place of a `Notification`; scored on the all-clear flag and the named entries; it writes no `Notification`, `Delivery` or live event (Story 17.7). [AMENDED 2026-11-03, Story 33.1, orchestrator unattended ruling, veto U-246]
    - **The session case.** A case of kind `command` may declare `session` (Story 41.14): `EvalIn` makes an eval-only `Session` row (no user, no hash) and asks the turn with that session as the request's `OwnerRef`, while the Item stays owner `eval`. It seeds a `forgotten` memory by sending `MemoryOps` a `SaveMemory` and a `ForgetMemory` (origin `system`). `TurnResult` keeps `HostData.memories`. `Finish` and `Start` drop the memories (`Memory.DropSession`), the conversation links of its turns (`Bridge.Purge.DropLinks`) and the row (`DropEval`). Its turns publish no live event and send no `Notification`: `BridgeSink` treats an evaluation session (`Data.Session.IsEval`) as no session. [ADDED 2026-11-09, Story 41.14]
  - A CI step fails on any `docker push` or registry sign-in. The AI Hub image never enters a cache or artifact. Fork pull requests never run on the IRIS runner, which is never the online demo server. CI and release scripts never run `docker compose config` without `--quiet`, or `docker inspect` without `--format`, because both print the values from `env_file` (S1).
